## User

Context here -- review the information and wait for more context: I developed two scripts: testing-sequence.R, and sampling-distributions.R. This was the design brief: I want to create a set of images that can illustrate the sequence of ideas, centered on thinking about sampling and measurement error, leading to thoughts about sampling distributions and frequentist null hypothesis significance testing. There will need to be two sets of figures: (1.) a series of icon-based images progressively showing one person responding to a computer-based data collection task (this is you being tested), your score is x=y, 2 x that image (we test two people), their scores are y1, y2, a grid of x times the image (this is a sample of people), we do not show the scores but my narrative says there are multiple scores (2.) a series of synthetic data-based computationally generated figures showing one score (a dot plot), two scores (two dots), a pile of dots (this would be based on ggdist dot geom objects designed to show a probability distribution as a set of dots not a density curve or a histogram), then a histogram showing a normal distribution, then a grid of histograms to represent multiple normal distributions. I need to be able to generate both sets of images parametrically so that (1.) the figure images should be capable of the same color or shape variation permitted previously, and I should be able to generate grids of as many figure images as required (2.) the synthetic data simulation based images should be capable of variation with respect to the mean and the SD of distributions. The basis for the synthetic data simulation is: (1.) measurement error framework y = a + bx_i + e_i where e_i can reflect measurement error, and errors e_1 ... e_n can be considered a random sample from a distribution mean mean 0 and standard deviation \sigma. Let's assume that the task for data collection is like the Shipley vocabulary test: participants respond to total 40 multiple choice questions; they can get 0-40 responses correct. Most of the time, adult university students might get responses distributed around 20-40 with a skew towards higher scores. Let's simplify things and assume that the distribution is normal. Let's not tie the simulations specifically to the Shipley but say that is it a vocabulary test of some kind where the observation or trial level response is the accuracy of response made by a participant to a test item. Let's say that there is some measurement error (the test is good but not perfect) so that if a participant did two equivalent alternate versions of the test, their scores would correlate quite highly (~ 80%) or if they did the same test twice, their scores would correlate quite highly (~80%) ignoring the effect of test-retest repetition priming. Let's say that if we did a series of studies, testing in each study a sample of participants, the distribution of scores would resemble the normal (more closely, for larger samples) but there is sampling error so that no distribution of scores would be exactly the same as the other. Let's say that the data generating process is capable of resulting in the simulation of a dataset in which outcome accuracy is correlated to a continuous numeric variable (e.g., age, or reading experience). The simulations should be set up so that the degree of correlation can be varied as a parameter from 0 to either negative or positive correlations. I think I need two sets of scripts, one to do the icon images (one person, two people, grid of people), a second to do data simulations and figure generation: dot, pile of dots, histogram, scatterplot. I want you to critically evaluate the scripts. I want to test out production before integration in my current working slides .qmd.

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_0155mK9T6Xxf7VT7oERK3Lvc"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 8.8KB, 214 lines, showing lines 1-214]\n1\t#' =======================================...[truncated, total 9836 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 8.8KB, 214 lines, showing lines 1-214]\n#' ============================================================\n#' testing-sequence.R\n#'\n#' Extends person-grid.R with a \"person at a computer\" composite icon,\n#' for the narrative sequence: one person doing the task -> two people\n#' doing the task -> a whole sample doing the task (grid, no scores\n#' shown). Same silhouette/age/disability/colour parametrization as\n#' person-grid.R carries straight over, including person-grid.R's two\n#' fill modes: \"diversity\" (sampled skin tones) or \"value\" (continuous\n#' diverging scale driven by a numeric score/effect per icon).\n#'\n#' The screen is sized and positioned to face the head (vertically\n#' centred on it, at a fixed horizontal offset) so the icon reads as\n#' \"a person looking at a screen\" rather than two unrelated shapes.\n#'\n#' Requires person-grid.R to be sourced first (same folder).\n#'\n#' Quarto usage:\n#'   ```{r}\n#'   source(\"person-grid.R\")\n#'   source(\"testing-sequence.R\")\n#'   testing_icons(n = 1, scores = 27)$plot\n#'   testing_icons(n = 2, scores = c(31, 24))$plot\n#'   testing_icons(n = 30, seed = 7)$plot   # no `scores` -> sample, no labels\n#'   # colour-by-score instead of sampled diversity:\n#'   testing_icons(n = 6, mode = \"value\", value = c(-2, -1, 0, 0.5, 1, 2))$plot\n#'   ```\n#' ============================================================\n\nlibrary(ggplot2)\n\n# ---- monitor/computer geometry -------------------------------------------\n\n#' Screen geometry in the same local coordinate frame as person_parts()\n#' (head centred at x = 0, y = 1.62), so it can be positioned relative\n#' to the head and scaled/offset identically to the person. Sized and\n#' vertically centred on the head so the pair reads as \"looking at a\n#' screen\" rather than a monitor floating near the feet.\nmonitor_parts \u003c- function(scale = 1, ox = 0, oy = 0,\n                           center_y = 1.62, x_offset = 0.80) {\n  bezel_hw \u003c- 0.40\n  bezel_hh \u003c- 0.34\n  inset \u003c- 0.05\n  neck_h \u003c- 0.10\n  neck_hw \u003c- 0.04\n  base_hw \u003c- 0.22\n  base_h \u003c- 0.05\n\n  bezel_top \u003c- center_y + bezel_hh\n  bezel_bottom \u003c- center_y - bezel_hh\n  neck_top \u003c- bezel_bottom\n  neck_bottom \u003c- neck_top - neck_h\n  base_top \u003c- neck_bottom\n  base_bottom \u003c- base_top - base_h\n\n  parts \u003c- list(\n    bezel  = data.frame(x = x_offset + c(-bezel_hw, bezel_hw, bezel_hw, -bezel_hw),\n                         y = c(bezel_top, bezel_top, bezel_bottom, bezel_bottom)),\n    screen = data.frame(x = x_offset + c(-bezel_hw + inset, bezel_hw - inset,\n                                          bezel_hw - inset, -bezel_hw + inset),\n                         y = c(bezel_top - inset, bezel_top - inset,\n                               bezel_bottom + inset, bezel_bottom + inset)),\n    # inverted-T stand: narrow neck down to a wider foot\n    neck   = data.frame(x = x_offset + c(-neck_hw, neck_hw, neck_hw, -neck_hw),\n                         y = c(neck_top, neck_top, neck_bottom, neck_bottom)),\n    base   = data.frame(x = x_offset + c(-base_hw, base_hw, base_hw, -base_hw),\n                         y = c(base_top, base_top, base_bottom, base_bottom))\n  )\n  out \u003c- lapply(names(parts), function(nm) {\n    p \u003c- parts[[nm]]\n    data.frame(part = nm, x = ox + scale * p$x, y = oy + scale * p$y)\n  })\n  do.call(rbind, out)\n}\n\n#' One person + screen, as a single composite polygon set with a `role`\n#' column (\"person\" vs \"monitor\") so they can be filled differently.\nperson_at_computer \u003c- function(kind = \"A\", age = \"adult\", disabled = FALSE,\n                                scale = 1, ox = 0, oy = 0,\n                                person_color = \"#3B6EA5\",\n                                screen_color = \"#CFE8FF\",\n                                frame_color = \"#555555\") {\n  p \u003c- person_parts(kind, age, disabled, scale, ox, oy)\n  p$role \u003c- \"person\"\n  p$fill \u003c- person_color\n\n  m \u003c- monitor_parts(scale = scale, ox = ox, oy = oy)\n  m$role \u003c- \"monitor\"\n  m$fill \u003c- ifelse(m$part == \"screen\", screen_color, frame_color)\n\n  rbind(p, m)\n}\n\n# ---- main sequence function -----------------------------------------------\n\n#' Draw n copies of \"person at computer\".\n#'\n#' @param n        how many icons (1 = single participant, 2 = a pair,\n#'                 larger = a sample).\n#' @param scores   optional numeric vector of length n; if supplied, each\n#'                 icon gets an \"x = \u003cscore>\" label underneath (used for\n#'                 the n = 1 and n = 2 steps). Leave NULL for the sample\n#'                 grid step, where individual scores aren't shown.\n#' @param ncol     icons per row; default is n itself for n \u003c= 4, else\n#'                 a compact grid.\n#' @param comp_kind, comp_age, disabled_prop, seed: same composition\n#'                 controls as person_grid().\n#' @param mode     \"diversity\" (default; sampled skin-tone fill, as\n#'                 before) or \"value\" (continuous diverging fill driven\n#'                 by `value`, e.g. to colour icons by score/effect).\n#' @param skin_tones hex colours to sample from when mode = \"diversity\".\n#' @param value    numeric vector of length n; required when\n#'                 mode = \"value\". 0 = the null/reference point (see\n#'                 `diverging_colors()` in person-grid.R).\n#' @return list(plot = ggplot object, data = polygon data frame)\ntesting_icons \u003c- function(n, scores = NULL, ncol = NULL,\n                           comp_kind = c(A = 0.45, B = 0.45, N = 0.10),\n                           comp_age = c(adult = 1.0),\n                           disabled_prop = 0,\n                           mode = c(\"diversity\", \"value\"),\n                           skin_tones = c(\"#4A3728\", \"#7A5230\", \"#B07A4E\",\n                                          \"#D9A066\", \"#F0C892\", \"#F5E1C8\"),\n                           value = NULL,\n                           seed = 1, dx = 1.9, dy = 2.5) {\n  mode \u003c- match.arg(mode)\n  if (!is.null(scores)) stopifnot(length(scores) == n)\n  if (is.null(ncol)) ncol \u003c- if (n \u003c= 4) n else min(6, ceiling(sqrt(n)))\n  nrow_ \u003c- ceiling(n / ncol)\n\n  kinds \u003c- sample_counts(n, comp_kind, seed)\n  ages \u003c- sample_counts(n, comp_age, seed + 1)\n  disabled \u003c- sample_counts(n, c(yes = disabled_prop, no = 1 - disabled_prop), seed + 2) == \"yes\"\n\n  if (mode == \"diversity\") {\n    set.seed(seed + 3)\n    colors \u003c- sample(skin_tones, n, replace = TRUE)\n  } else {\n    stopifnot(\"`value` must be supplied and length n when mode = 'value'\" =\n                !is.null(value) && length(value) == n)\n    colors \u003c- diverging_colors(value)\n  }\n\n  polys \u003c- vector(\"list\", n)\n  labels \u003c- vector(\"list\", n)\n  for (i in seq_len(n)) {\n    r \u003c- (i - 1) %/% ncol\n    c \u003c- (i - 1) %% ncol\n    ox \u003c- c * dx\n    oy \u003c- -r * dy\n    p \u003c- person_at_computer(kinds[i], ages[i], disabled[i], 1.0, ox, oy, colors[i])\n    p$icon_id \u003c- i\n    p$poly_id \u003c- paste(i, p$part, p$role)\n    polys[[i]] \u003c- p\n    if (!is.null(scores)) {\n      labels[[i]] \u003c- data.frame(x = ox + 0.2, y = oy - 0.35,\n                                 label = paste0(\"x = \", scores[i]))\n    }\n  }\n  df \u003c- do.call(rbind, polys)\n\n  plot \u003c- ggplot(df, aes(x, y, group = poly_id, fill = fill)) +\n    geom_polygon() +\n    scale_fill_identity() +\n    coord_equal() +\n    theme_void()\n\n  if (!is.null(scores)) {\n    lab_df \u003c- do.call(rbind, labels)\n    plot \u003c- plot + geom_text(data = lab_df, aes(x, y, label = label),\n                              inherit.aes = FALSE, size = 5)\n  }\n\n  list(plot = plot, data = df)\n}\n\n#' A grid of icon-grids, one per \"study\" (facet_wrap(~study)) -- the icon\n#' analogue of sampling-distributions.R's plot_histogram_grid()/\n#' plot_dotpile_grid(), for showing \"many studies, each with their own\n#' independently-sampled group of people\".\n#'\n#' @param n_studies    number of study panels.\n#' @param n_per_study  number of icons (people) per study panel.\n#' @param ncol         panels per row (passed to facet_wrap).\n#' @param seed         base seed; study s uses seed + s so each panel's\n#'                     sample is independently drawn but reproducible.\n#' @param ...          passed through to testing_icons() for each panel's\n#'                     composition/colour controls (comp_kind, comp_age,\n#'                     disabled_prop, mode, skin_tones, value, dx, dy).\n#'                     `scores` isn't supported here (no labels, as for\n#'                     the single-study sample grid).\n#' @return list(plot = ggplot object, data = polygon data frame)\ntesting_icons_studies \u003c- function(n_studies, n_per_study, ncol = NULL,\n                                   seed = 1, ...) {\n  panels \u003c- vector(\"list\", n_studies)\n  for (s in seq_len(n_studies)) {\n    g \u003c- testing_icons(n = n_per_study, seed = seed + s, ...)\n    g$data$study \u003c- paste(\"Study\", s)\n    panels[[s]] \u003c- g$data\n  }\n  df \u003c- do.call(rbind, panels)\n  df$poly_id \u003c- paste(df$study, df$poly_id)\n\n  plot \u003c- ggplot(df, aes(x, y, group = poly_id, fill = fill)) +\n    geom_polygon() +\n    scale_fill_identity() +\n    coord_equal() +\n    facet_wrap(~study, ncol = ncol) +\n    theme_void() +\n    theme(strip.text = element_text(face = \"bold\"),\n          panel.spacing = unit(2, \"lines\"))\n\n  list(plot = plot, data = df)\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":12}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_011rEZV4geWiLeionXWnWTo2"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 20.2KB, 434 lines, showing lines 1-434]\n1\t#' ======================================...[truncated, total 22404 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 20.2KB, 434 lines, showing lines 1-434]\n#' ============================================================\n#' sampling-distributions.R\n#'\n#' Simulates scores on a hypothetical 0-40 item vocabulary test from a\n#' trial level up, and draws the figure sequence: one dot -> two dots ->\n#' a \"pile of dots\" showing a sample's distribution -> a histogram -> a\n#' grid of histograms (repeated studies) -> a scatterplot against a\n#' continuous covariate.\n#'\n#' Model (trial level up, so every observed score is *structurally*\n#' bounded to [0, n_items] -- no post-hoc clipping is needed):\n#'\n#'   Ability (logit scale):  theta_i     ~ N(theta_mu, theta_sigma)\n#'   Per-item probability:   p_i         =  plogis(theta_i)\n#'   Trial-level responses:  Y_i1..Y_i40 ~  Bernoulli(p_i)   (independent)\n#'   Observed score:         score_i     =  sum_j Y_ij        in {0,...,40}\n#'\n#' `theta_mu` and `theta_sigma` aren't set directly -- they're solved\n#' numerically (see `calibrate_ability()`) so that the *emergent* mean\n#' score matches `mu`, and the emergent parallel-forms reliability\n#' (corr(score, score') for two independent administrations drawn from\n#' the same theta_i, i.e. the same person re-tested) matches\n#' `reliability`. This mirrors real test theory: for a fixed test\n#' length, how reliable a test is and how spread out scores are, are\n#' linked to each other and to the mean, not independently free knobs --\n#' fixing `mu` and one of {`reliability`, `sd_true`} determines the\n#' other. `calibrate_ability()` (and therefore `simulate_vocab_data()` /\n#' `simulate_parallel_forms()`) accepts either: pass `reliability` (the\n#' default) when a figure is about repeatability, or pass `sd_true`\n#' directly when a figure needs independent control of spread (e.g. two\n#' panels with the same mean but visibly different spread). Whichever\n#' one wasn't the calibration target is still available afterwards as an\n#' emergent value via `calibrate_ability()$moments`.\n#'\n#' `r_with_x` is specified on the *observed*-score scale, i.e. it's\n#' (approximately) the correlation you'll actually see in\n#' `plot_scatter()`. Because measurement error attenuates any\n#' correlation involving a noisy observed variable, hitting a target\n#' observed-score correlation requires a *larger* correlation between\n#' the underlying ability and x; classical test theory gives\n#' corr(observed, true ability) = sqrt(reliability), so that attenuation\n#' is inverted internally. If `r_with_x` exceeds what's achievable at\n#' the requested `reliability` (i.e. `abs(r_with_x) > sqrt(reliability)`),\n#' it's capped and a warning is issued.\n#'\n#' Requires: ggplot2, ggdist (for the dot-pile figure).\n#'\n#' Quarto usage:\n#'   ```{r}\n#'   source(\"sampling-distributions.R\")\n#'   d \u003c- simulate_vocab_data(n = 40, seed = 1)\n#'   plot_dots(d$observed_score[1])\n#'   plot_dots(d$observed_score[1:2])\n#'   plot_dotpile(d$observed_score)\n#'   plot_histogram(d$observed_score)\n#'   plot_histogram_grid(simulate_studies(6, 40, seed = 2))\n#'   plot_scatter(simulate_vocab_data(n = 60, r_with_x = 0.5, seed = 3))\n#'   ```\n#' ============================================================\n\nlibrary(ggplot2)\n\n# ---- calibration ------------------------------------------------------\n\n#' Expectation of `f(theta)` under theta ~ N(theta_mu, theta_sigma),\n#' via numerical integration (base R, no extra dependency).\nexpect_theta \u003c- function(f, theta_mu, theta_sigma) {\n  integrate(function(theta) f(theta) * dnorm(theta, theta_mu, theta_sigma),\n            lower = theta_mu - 10 * theta_sigma,\n            upper = theta_mu + 10 * theta_sigma)$value\n}\n\n#' Moments of the observed score implied by a given (theta_mu,\n#' theta_sigma, n_items), under the trial-level model described above.\n#' `var_error` uses the binomial variance n*p*(1-p) as the expected\n#' within-person sampling variance across the 40 items.\nimplied_moments \u003c- function(theta_mu, theta_sigma, n_items) {\n  p_bar  \u003c- expect_theta(plogis, theta_mu, theta_sigma)\n  p2_bar \u003c- expect_theta(function(th) plogis(th)^2, theta_mu, theta_sigma)\n  pq_bar \u003c- expect_theta(function(th) plogis(th) * (1 - plogis(th)), theta_mu, theta_sigma)\n\n  mean_score  \u003c- n_items * p_bar\n  var_true    \u003c- n_items^2 * (p2_bar - p_bar^2)\n  var_error   \u003c- n_items * pq_bar\n  reliability \u003c- var_true / (var_true + var_error)\n\n  list(mean_score = mean_score, var_true = var_true, var_error = var_error,\n       reliability = reliability, sd_true = sqrt(var_true),\n       sd_total = sqrt(var_true + var_error))\n}\n\n#' Solve for the (theta_mu, theta_sigma) of the ability distribution\n#' that make the trial-level model's emergent mean score match `mu`,\n#' and match *either* a target `reliability` *or* a target `sd_true`\n#' (the emergent SD of the \"true\"/expected score across people) --\n#' whichever is supplied. These two are not independently settable: for\n#' a fixed mu and n_items, specifying one determines the other, so pick\n#' whichever is more natural for the figure you're building (reliability\n#' for \"how repeatable is this test\", sd_true for \"how spread out is\n#' this population\").\n#'\n#' @param mu           target mean observed score (0 - n_items).\n#' @param reliability  target parallel-forms / test-retest reliability\n#'                      (0-1, exclusive). Used only when `sd_true` is\n#'                      NULL. Values above about 0.99 are not achievable\n#'                      for a 40-item test (they'd require an implausibly\n#'                      large ability spread) and raise an informative\n#'                      error rather than silently returning nonsense.\n#' @param sd_true      target SD, on the 0-n_items score scale, of\n#'                      people's expected/\"true\" scores (i.e. the\n#'                      between-person spread, excluding item-sampling\n#'                      noise). When supplied, this is calibrated\n#'                      directly and `reliability` is ignored. Must be\n#'                      less than the theoretical ceiling\n#'                      `sqrt(mu * (n_items - mu))` (approached only as\n#'                      the ability spread goes to infinity); requesting\n#'                      a value at or above that raises an informative\n#'                      error.\n#' @param n_items       number of trials/items (default 40).\n#' @return list(theta_mu, theta_sigma, moments) -- `moments` is the\n#'   full set of implied moments at the solution, including the\n#'   emergent `sd_true`/`sd_total`/`reliability` (whichever wasn't the\n#'   target), for inspection.\ncalibrate_ability \u003c- function(mu, reliability = 0.80, sd_true = NULL, n_items = 40) {\n  stopifnot(\"`mu` must be strictly between 0 and `n_items`\" = mu > 0 && mu \u003c n_items)\n\n  theta_mu_for_sigma \u003c- function(theta_sigma) {\n    uniroot(function(theta_mu) implied_moments(theta_mu, theta_sigma, n_items)$mean_score - mu,\n            lower = -15, upper = 15, extendInt = \"yes\")$root\n  }\n\n  # Robustly solve for the theta_sigma that zeroes `gap_fn`, widening the\n  # search bracket by hand rather than relying on uniroot's own\n  # extendInt = \"yes\" -- which occasionally overshoots into a theta_sigma\n  # so large that the inner theta_mu search and integrate() start\n  # returning NaN, or (near mid-scale mu) integrate() itself reports a\n  # divergent integral. A target that's only reachable out there implies\n  # an implausibly large, numerically unstable ability spread, so both\n  # failure modes are treated the same way: fail with an informative\n  # message instead of propagating a cryptic numerical error.\n  solve_theta_sigma \u003c- function(gap_fn, fail_message) {\n    gap_at \u003c- function(theta_sigma) tryCatch(gap_fn(theta_sigma), error = function(e) NA_real_)\n    lower \u003c- 0.02\n    upper \u003c- 8\n    max_upper \u003c- 128\n    g \u003c- gap_at(upper)\n    while ((is.na(g) || g \u003c 0) && upper \u003c max_upper) {\n      upper \u003c- upper * 2\n      g \u003c- gap_at(upper)\n    }\n    if (is.na(g) || g \u003c 0) stop(fail_message)\n    tryCatch(uniroot(gap_fn, lower = lower, upper = upper)$root,\n             error = function(e) stop(fail_message))\n  }\n\n  if (!is.null(sd_true)) {\n    stopifnot(\"`sd_true` must be positive\" = sd_true > 0)\n    max_sd \u003c- sqrt(mu * (n_items - mu))\n    if (sd_true >= max_sd) {\n      stop(sprintf(\n        paste(\"sd_true = %.2f is not achievable at mu = %.1f with\",\n              \"n_items = %d: the theoretical ceiling (approached only as\",\n              \"the ability spread -> infinity) is %.2f. Try a smaller sd_true.\"),\n        sd_true, mu, n_items, max_sd\n      ))\n    }\n    sd_true_gap \u003c- function(theta_sigma) {\n      theta_mu \u003c- theta_mu_for_sigma(theta_sigma)\n      implied_moments(theta_mu, theta_sigma, n_items)$sd_true - sd_true\n    }\n    theta_sigma \u003c- solve_theta_sigma(sd_true_gap, sprintf(\n      paste(\"sd_true = %.2f is not achievable at mu = %.1f with\",\n            \"n_items = %d (it would require a numerically unstable\",\n            \"ability spread, even though it's below the theoretical\",\n            \"ceiling of %.2f). Try a smaller sd_true.\"),\n      sd_true, mu, n_items, max_sd\n    ))\n  } else {\n    stopifnot(\n      \"`reliability` must be strictly between 0 and 1\" =\n        reliability > 0 && reliability \u003c 1\n    )\n    reliability_gap \u003c- function(theta_sigma) {\n      theta_mu \u003c- theta_mu_for_sigma(theta_sigma)\n      implied_moments(theta_mu, theta_sigma, n_items)$reliability - reliability\n    }\n    theta_sigma \u003c- solve_theta_sigma(reliability_gap, sprintf(\n      paste(\"reliability = %.3f is not achievable at mu = %.1f with\",\n            \"n_items = %d (it would require an implausibly large,\",\n            \"numerically unstable ability spread). Try a lower\",\n            \"reliability (\u003c= ~0.99 is usually safe) or a larger n_items.\"),\n      reliability, mu, n_items\n    ))\n  }\n\n  theta_mu \u003c- theta_mu_for_sigma(theta_sigma)\n  list(theta_mu = theta_mu, theta_sigma = theta_sigma,\n       moments = implied_moments(theta_mu, theta_sigma, n_items))\n}\n\n# ---- simulation -----------------------------------------------------------\n\n#' Simulate one sample of vocabulary-test scores, trial level up.\n#'\n#' @param n            sample size.\n#' @param mu           target mean observed score (0-40 scale).\n#' @param reliability  target parallel-forms / test-retest reliability\n#'                      (0-1); jointly with `mu` and `n_items`, this\n#'                      determines the emergent between-person ability\n#'                      spread (see the file header). Ignored if\n#'                      `sd_true` is supplied.\n#' @param sd_true      alternate calibration target: the SD (0-n_items\n#'                      scale) of people's expected/\"true\" scores,\n#'                      calibrated directly instead of `reliability` --\n#'                      use this when a figure needs independent control\n#'                      of spread rather than of repeatability. See\n#'                      `calibrate_ability()` for the achievable range.\n#' @param r_with_x     desired correlation between the *observed* score\n#'                      and the covariate x, from -1 to 1 (0 = no\n#'                      relationship). Capped at sqrt(reliability) if\n#'                      the requested value isn't achievable, with a\n#'                      warning (`reliability` here is the emergent\n#'                      value when calibrating via `sd_true`).\n#' @param x            optional pre-specified covariate vector (length n);\n#'                      if NULL, one is drawn from N(x_mean, x_sd).\n#' @param x_mean, x_sd  mean/SD used to generate x when not supplied.\n#' @param n_items      number of trials/items (default 40, matching a\n#'                      0-40 item test).\n#' @param seed         for reproducibility.\n#'\n#' @return a data frame: id, x, theta (ability, logit scale), p_correct\n#'   (per-item probability implied by theta), observed_score (integer,\n#'   always in [0, n_items] by construction).\nsimulate_vocab_data \u003c- function(n, mu = 30, reliability = 0.80, sd_true = NULL, r_with_x = 0,\n                                 x = NULL, x_mean = 0, x_sd = 1,\n                                 n_items = 40, seed = NULL) {\n  if (!is.null(seed)) set.seed(seed)\n  if (!is.null(sd_true) && !missing(reliability)) {\n    warning(paste(\n      \"Both `reliability` and `sd_true` were supplied to\",\n      \"simulate_vocab_data(); they aren't independently settable\",\n      \"(fixing mu and sd_true determines reliability, and vice versa).\",\n      \"Using `sd_true` and ignoring `reliability`.\"\n    ))\n  }\n  cal \u003c- calibrate_ability(mu, reliability = reliability, sd_true = sd_true, n_items = n_items)\n  reliability \u003c- cal$moments$reliability\n\n  if (is.null(x)) x \u003c- rnorm(n, x_mean, x_sd)\n\n  max_r \u003c- sqrt(reliability)\n  if (abs(r_with_x) > max_r) {\n    warning(sprintf(\n      paste(\"r_with_x = %.2f is not achievable at reliability = %.2f\",\n            \"(measurement error caps the observed-score correlation at\",\n            \"about %.2f); using %.2f instead.\"),\n      r_with_x, reliability, max_r, sign(r_with_x) * max_r\n    ))\n    r_with_x \u003c- sign(r_with_x) * max_r\n  }\n  if (r_with_x != 0 && n \u003c 2) {\n    warning(\"r_with_x is not meaningful for n \u003c 2 (correlation is undefined for a single observation); ignoring it.\")\n    r_with_x \u003c- 0\n  }\n  r_theta_x \u003c- if (r_with_x == 0) 0 else r_with_x / max_r\n\n  # Only standardise x (and only use it) when it's actually needed: with\n  # r_theta_x == 0, scale(x) may be NaN (e.g. n \u003c 2, or a constant x) and\n  # 0 * NaN is NaN in R, not 0 -- multiplying it in unconditionally would\n  # silently turn every theta (and hence every score) into NA.\n  z \u003c- rnorm(n)\n  if (r_theta_x == 0) {\n    theta_z \u003c- z\n  } else {\n    xz \u003c- as.numeric(scale(x))\n    if (anyNA(xz)) {\n      warning(\"x has zero variance (e.g. all identical values); r_with_x cannot be induced. Ignoring r_with_x.\")\n      theta_z \u003c- z\n    } else {\n      theta_z \u003c- r_theta_x * xz + sqrt(1 - r_theta_x^2) * z\n    }\n  }\n  theta \u003c- cal$theta_mu + cal$theta_sigma * theta_z\n  p_correct \u003c- plogis(theta)\n\n  observed_score \u003c- rbinom(n, size = n_items, prob = p_correct)\n\n  data.frame(id = seq_len(n), x = x, theta = theta, p_correct = p_correct,\n             observed_score = observed_score)\n}\n\n#' Simulate `n_studies` independent samples of size `n_per_study`, for\n#' the \"no two samples look identical\" histogram-grid figure.\n#' Returns one long data frame with a `study` id column.\nsimulate_studies \u003c- function(n_studies, n_per_study, seed = NULL, ...) {\n  if (!is.null(seed)) set.seed(seed)\n  out \u003c- lapply(seq_len(n_studies), function(s) {\n    d \u003c- simulate_vocab_data(n_per_study, seed = NULL, ...)\n    d$study \u003c- paste(\"Study\", s)\n    d\n  })\n  do.call(rbind, out)\n}\n\n#' Two independent administrations (parallel forms / test-retest) per\n#' person, sharing the same ability theta_i, to check/demonstrate the\n#' calibrated reliability directly (corr(form1, form2) ~ reliability).\n#' @param sd_true  alternate calibration target in place of `reliability`\n#'                  -- see `simulate_vocab_data()` / `calibrate_ability()`.\nsimulate_parallel_forms \u003c- function(n, mu = 30, reliability = 0.80, sd_true = NULL,\n                                     n_items = 40, seed = NULL) {\n  if (!is.null(seed)) set.seed(seed)\n  if (!is.null(sd_true) && !missing(reliability)) {\n    warning(paste(\n      \"Both `reliability` and `sd_true` were supplied to\",\n      \"simulate_parallel_forms(); they aren't independently settable.\",\n      \"Using `sd_true` and ignoring `reliability`.\"\n    ))\n  }\n  cal \u003c- calibrate_ability(mu, reliability = reliability, sd_true = sd_true, n_items = n_items)\n  theta \u003c- rnorm(n, cal$theta_mu, cal$theta_sigma)\n  p_correct \u003c- plogis(theta)\n  data.frame(id = seq_len(n), theta = theta, p_correct = p_correct,\n             form1 = rbinom(n, n_items, p_correct),\n             form2 = rbinom(n, n_items, p_correct))\n}\n\n# ---- figures ----------------------------------------------------------\n\n#' One or two scores plotted as points on a number line.\nplot_dots \u003c- function(scores, xlim = c(0, 40)) {\n  # y nudged just above 0 so the dot rests on top of the axis line\n  # rather than being bisected by it.\n  df \u003c- data.frame(score = scores, y = 0.04)\n  ggplot(df, aes(score, y)) +\n    geom_point(size = 6, color = \"#3B6EA5\") +\n    scale_x_continuous(name = \"Score (items correct)\") +\n    scale_y_continuous(limits = c(0, 0.5), expand = expansion(mult = c(0, 0.1))) +\n    coord_cartesian(xlim = xlim) +\n    theme_minimal() +\n    theme(axis.title.y = element_blank(), axis.text.y = element_blank(),\n          panel.grid.major.y = element_blank(), panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),\n          axis.line.x = element_line(color = \"black\", linewidth = 0.9),\n          axis.ticks.x = element_line(color = \"black\"))\n}\n\n#' A sample's scores as a \"pile of dots\" (ggdist::stat_dots) -- shows\n#' the distribution as discrete points, not a smoothed density/histogram.\nplot_dotpile \u003c- function(scores, xlim = c(0, 40), ...) {\n  if (!requireNamespace(\"ggdist\", quietly = TRUE)) {\n    stop(\"Package 'ggdist' is required for plot_dotpile(). Install with install.packages('ggdist').\")\n  }\n  df \u003c- data.frame(score = scores)\n  ggplot(df, aes(x = score)) +\n    ggdist::stat_dots(color = \"#3B6EA5\", fill = \"#3B6EA5\", ...) +\n    scale_x_continuous(name = \"Score (items correct)\") +\n    coord_cartesian(xlim = xlim) +\n    theme_minimal() +\n    theme(axis.title.y = element_blank(), axis.text.y = element_blank(),\n          panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),\n          axis.line.x = element_line(color = \"black\", linewidth = 0.9),\n          axis.ticks.x = element_line(color = \"black\"))\n}\n\n#' A grid of \"pile of dots\" distributions, one per \"study\" -- the\n#' ggdist::stat_dots analogue of plot_histogram_grid(), for showing\n#' sampling variability as discrete points rather than binned counts.\n#' @param data a data frame with a `study` and `observed_score` column,\n#'   e.g. from simulate_studies().\n#' @param binwidth dot diameter in data units (score points). ggdist's\n#'   default computes a separate binwidth per facet panel, sized to\n#'   stack that panel's tallest pile within the available height --\n#'   which makes dots different sizes across panels. Fixing binwidth\n#'   here keeps every panel's dots the same size.\nplot_dotpile_grid \u003c- function(data, value_col = \"observed_score\",\n                               xlim = c(0, 40), ncol = NULL, binwidth = 2.5, ...) {\n  if (!requireNamespace(\"ggdist\", quietly = TRUE)) {\n    stop(\"Package 'ggdist' is required for plot_dotpile_grid(). Install with install.packages('ggdist').\")\n  }\n  data$.value \u003c- data[[value_col]]\n  ggplot(data, aes(x = .value)) +\n    ggdist::stat_dots(color = \"#3B6EA5\", fill = \"#3B6EA5\", binwidth = binwidth, ...) +\n    scale_x_continuous(name = \"Score (items correct)\") +\n    coord_cartesian(xlim = xlim) +\n    facet_wrap(~study, ncol = ncol) +\n    theme_minimal() +\n    theme(axis.title.y = element_blank(), axis.text.y = element_blank(),\n          panel.grid.minor = element_blank(),\n          strip.text = element_text(face = \"bold\"),\n          panel.spacing = unit(2, \"lines\"),\n          axis.line.x = element_line(color = \"black\", linewidth = 0.9),\n          axis.ticks.x = element_line(color = \"black\"))\n}\n\n#' A histogram of one sample's scores.\nplot_histogram \u003c- function(scores, binwidth = 1, xlim = c(0, 40)) {\n  df \u003c- data.frame(score = scores)\n  ggplot(df, aes(x = score)) +\n    geom_histogram(binwidth = binwidth, fill = \"#3B6EA5\", color = \"white\") +\n    scale_x_continuous(name = \"Score (items correct)\") +\n    coord_cartesian(xlim = xlim) +\n    ylab(\"Count\") +\n    theme_minimal()\n}\n\n#' A grid of histograms, one per \"study\", to show sampling variability\n#' across repeated samples from the same population.\n#' @param data a data frame with a `study` and `observed_score` column,\n#'   e.g. from simulate_studies().\nplot_histogram_grid \u003c- function(data, value_col = \"observed_score\",\n                                 binwidth = 1, xlim = c(0, 40), ncol = NULL) {\n  data$.value \u003c- data[[value_col]]\n  ggplot(data, aes(x = .value)) +\n    geom_histogram(binwidth = binwidth, fill = \"#3B6EA5\", color = \"white\") +\n    scale_x_continuous(name = \"Score (items correct)\") +\n    coord_cartesian(xlim = xlim) +\n    ylab(\"Count\") +\n    facet_wrap(~study, ncol = ncol) +\n    theme_minimal() +\n    theme(strip.text = element_text(face = \"bold\"))\n}\n\n#' Scatterplot of score against the continuous covariate, with a fitted\n#' line, to illustrate the (settable) correlation.\nplot_scatter \u003c- function(data, x_col = \"x\", y_col = \"observed_score\", show_fit = TRUE) {\n  df \u003c- data.frame(x = data[[x_col]], y = data[[y_col]])\n  p \u003c- ggplot(df, aes(x, y)) +\n    geom_point(color = \"#3B6EA5\", alpha = 0.8) +\n    xlab(x_col) + ylab(\"Score (items correct)\") +\n    theme_minimal()\n  if (show_fit) p \u003c- p + geom_smooth(method = \"lm\", se = TRUE, color = \"#B23A48\")\n  p\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":5}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01PJVG3RmMSPYX9wWomgFDCW"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 11.5KB, 280 lines, showing lines 1-280]\n1\t#' ======================================...[truncated, total 12826 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 11.5KB, 280 lines, showing lines 1-280]\n#' ============================================================\n#' person-grid.R\n#'\n#' A parametrized \"restroom-sign style\" pictogram generator, built with\n#' plain polygons in ggplot2 (no external icon fonts, so it renders\n#' identically everywhere Quarto builds the book).\n#'\n#' Each icon is assembled from a few flat shapes:\n#'   - head          (circle)\n#'   - torso         (trapezoid)\n#'   - legs          (\"A\" = trouser silhouette, \"B\" = skirt silhouette,\n#'                     \"N\" = half trouser / half skirt -- an inclusive,\n#'                     non-binary-coded silhouette)\n#'   - wheelchair    (swapped in for the legs when disabled = TRUE)\n#'   - cane          (added when age = \"elder\")\n#'   - overall scale (reduced when age = \"child\")\n#'\n#' Colour is decided by ONE of two modes, chosen by whether you pass\n#' `value=`:\n#'   mode = \"diversity\" (default): each icon's fill is sampled from a\n#'          discrete set of skin tones -- for showing sample diversity.\n#'   mode = \"value\": each icon's fill is placed on a continuous,\n#'          symmetric diverging scale (dark negative -> light null ->\n#'          dark positive) driven by a numeric `value` per icon -- for\n#'          showing an effect size, a score, a probability, etc.\n#'\n#' Use in a Quarto R chunk:\n#'\n#'   ```{r}\n#'   #| label: fig-diversity-sample\n#'   #| fig-cap: \"A hypothetical study sample\"\n#'   #| fig-alt: !expr diversity_grid$alt_text\n#'   source(\"person-grid.R\")\n#'   diversity_grid \u003c- person_grid(n = 30, seed = 7)\n#'   diversity_grid$plot\n#'   ```\n#' ============================================================\n\nlibrary(ggplot2)\n\n# ---- low-level geometry -------------------------------------------------\n\ncircle_pts \u003c- function(cx, cy, r, n = 24) {\n  t \u003c- seq(0, 2 * pi, length.out = n + 1)[-(n + 1)]\n  data.frame(x = cx + r * cos(t), y = cy + r * sin(t))\n}\n\n#' Build the list of sub-polygons for one icon, in local coordinates,\n#' then offset by (ox, oy) and multiplied by `scale`.\nperson_parts \u003c- function(kind = c(\"A\", \"B\", \"N\"),\n                          age = c(\"adult\", \"child\", \"elder\"),\n                          disabled = FALSE,\n                          scale = 1, ox = 0, oy = 0) {\n  kind \u003c- match.arg(kind)\n  age \u003c- match.arg(age)\n  parts \u003c- list()\n\n  # head\n  parts[[\"head\"]] \u003c- circle_pts(0, 1.62, 0.30)\n\n  if (disabled) {\n    # Redesigned for legibility at small sizes: one large, unbroken wheel\n    # (no small second circle to be mistaken for a stray dot), seated\n    # directly under the torso with no gap, plus a single low footrest\n    # peg. Fewer, bigger shapes read better than several small ones once\n    # icons are shrunk down in a grid.\n    parts[[\"torso\"]] \u003c- data.frame(\n      x = c(-0.30, 0.30, 0.30, -0.30),\n      y = c(1.28, 1.28, 0.62, 0.62)\n    )\n    parts[[\"wheel\"]] \u003c- circle_pts(0.00, 0.28, 0.34, n = 32)\n    parts[[\"footrest\"]] \u003c- data.frame(\n      x = c(0.34, 0.50, 0.50, 0.34),\n      y = c(0.02, 0.02, 0.12, 0.12)\n    )\n  } else {\n    parts[[\"torso\"]] \u003c- data.frame(\n      x = c(-0.34, 0.34, 0.20, -0.20),\n      y = c(1.30, 1.30, 0.72, 0.72)\n    )\n\n    if (kind == \"A\") {           # trouser legs: two bars with a slim gap\n      parts[[\"leg_l\"]] \u003c- data.frame(x = c(-0.20, -0.03, -0.06, -0.20),\n                                      y = c(0.72, 0.72, 0.00, 0.00))\n      parts[[\"leg_r\"]] \u003c- data.frame(x = c(0.03, 0.20, 0.20, 0.06),\n                                      y = c(0.72, 0.72, 0.00, 0.00))\n    } else if (kind == \"B\") {    # skirt\n      parts[[\"legs\"]] \u003c- data.frame(x = c(-0.20, 0.20, 0.46, -0.46),\n                                     y = c(0.72, 0.72, 0.00, 0.00))\n    } else if (kind == \"N\") {    # half trouser (left) / half skirt (right)\n      parts[[\"leg_l\"]] \u003c- data.frame(x = c(-0.20, -0.02, -0.05, -0.20),\n                                      y = c(0.72, 0.72, 0.00, 0.00))\n      parts[[\"legs_skirt_half\"]] \u003c- data.frame(x = c(0.00, 0.20, 0.46, 0.00),\n                                                y = c(0.72, 0.72, 0.00, 0.00))\n    }\n\n    if (age == \"elder\") {\n      parts[[\"cane\"]] \u003c- data.frame(x = c(0.36, 0.40, 0.58, 0.54),\n                                     y = c(0.78, 0.78, 0.02, 0.02))\n    }\n  }\n\n  # apply scale (child = smaller) then offset into the grid position\n  out \u003c- lapply(names(parts), function(nm) {\n    p \u003c- parts[[nm]]\n    data.frame(part = nm, x = ox + scale * p$x, y = oy + scale * p$y)\n  })\n  do.call(rbind, out)\n}\n\n# ---- composition sampling -----------------------------------------------\n\n#' Largest-remainder sampling: turns a named vector of proportions into\n#' exactly `n` labels, hitting the target proportions as closely as\n#' integer counts allow, then shuffles their order.\nsample_counts \u003c- function(n, props, seed) {\n  stopifnot(\n    \"`props` must have non-negative values, with at least one positive\" =\n      all(props >= 0) && sum(props) > 0\n  )\n  props \u003c- props / sum(props)\n  set.seed(seed)\n  raw \u003c- props * n\n  counts \u003c- floor(raw)\n  remainder \u003c- n - sum(counts)\n  if (remainder > 0) {\n    order \u003c- order(-(raw - counts))\n    for (i in seq_len(remainder)) {\n      idx \u003c- order[((i - 1) %% length(order)) + 1]\n      counts[idx] \u003c- counts[idx] + 1\n    }\n  }\n  sample(rep(names(props), counts))\n}\n\n# ---- colour -----------------------------------------------------------\n\n#' Map a numeric value to a symmetric diverging colour, vivid at both\n#' extremes and light at zero (the \"null effect\" point). `value` need\n#' not be centred on the data -- 0 is always treated as the reference\n#' point, so effect sizes / differences map intuitively.\n#'\n#' Default endpoints are blue/orange from the Okabe-Ito colour-blind-safe\n#' palette (distinguishable under deuteranopia, protanopia, and\n#' tritanopia) rather than a red/blue or red/green diverging scale.\ndiverging_colors \u003c- function(value, neg = \"#0072B2\", mid = \"#F2F1EC\", pos = \"#E69F00\") {\n  span \u003c- max(abs(value), 1e-9)\n  tt \u003c- value / span                     # -1 .. 1, 0 = null\n  ramp_lo \u003c- grDevices::colorRamp(c(neg, mid))\n  ramp_hi \u003c- grDevices::colorRamp(c(mid, pos))\n  to_hex \u003c- function(m) grDevices::rgb(m[, 1], m[, 2], m[, 3], maxColorValue = 255)\n  vapply(tt, function(t) {\n    if (t \u003c= 0) to_hex(ramp_lo(t + 1))\n    else        to_hex(ramp_hi(t))\n  }, character(1))\n}\n\n# ---- automatic grid shape -----------------------------------------------\n\n#' Pick the `ncol` (icons per row) that makes an n-icon grid's bounding\n#' box (`ncol * dx` wide by `ceiling(n / ncol) * dy` tall) match a target\n#' width:height `aspect` ratio as closely as possible -- e.g. so the\n#' figure fills a 16:9 slide with minimal leftover margin either way.\nchoose_ncol \u003c- function(n, dx, dy, aspect) {\n  ncols \u003c- seq_len(n)\n  nrows \u003c- ceiling(n / ncols)\n  grid_ratio \u003c- (ncols * dx) / (nrows * dy)\n  # log-ratio distance treats \"twice too wide\" and \"twice too tall\"\n  # as equally bad, so it doesn't systematically favour wide grids.\n  ncols[which.min(abs(log(grid_ratio / aspect)))]\n}\n\n# ---- main function ------------------------------------------------------\n\n#' Build a grid of person pictograms.\n#'\n#' @param n           number of icons.\n#' @param ncol        icons per row (rows are computed from n). Leave as\n#'                    `\"auto\"` (the default) to pick the layout that best\n#'                    fills a window of the given `aspect` ratio, or pass\n#'                    an integer to fix it yourself.\n#' @param aspect      target width:height ratio used only when\n#'                    `ncol = \"auto\"`. Defaults to 16/9. Set this to match\n#'                    your actual slide size (e.g. `revealjs`'s default\n#'                    is 960/700; a custom `width`/`height` in the format\n#'                    YAML should be reflected here) so the grid fills\n#'                    the slide with as little leftover margin as\n#'                    possible.\n#' @param comp_kind   named proportions for silhouette: \"A\" (trouser-\n#'                    coded), \"B\" (skirt-coded), \"N\" (half/half --\n#'                    reads as a non-binary / trans-inclusive icon).\n#' @param comp_age    named proportions for \"adult\", \"child\", \"elder\".\n#' @param disabled_prop proportion of icons shown using the wheelchair\n#'                    silhouette.\n#' @param mode        \"diversity\" (discrete skin-tone fill, sampled) or\n#'                    \"value\" (continuous diverging fill driven by\n#'                    `value`).\n#' @param skin_tones  hex colours to sample from when mode = \"diversity\".\n#' @param value       numeric vector of length n; required when\n#'                    mode = \"value\". 0 = the null/reference point.\n#' @param seed        for reproducibility.\n#'\n#' @return a list with `plot` (a ggplot object), `data` (the full\n#'   polygon data frame, if you want to customise further), `meta`\n#'   (per-icon kind/age/disabled/fill), and `alt_text` (a plain-language\n#'   summary of the composition, generated from the same sampling used\n#'   to draw the icons -- pass it straight into `fig-alt`).\nperson_grid \u003c- function(n = 30, ncol = \"auto\", aspect = 16 / 9,\n                         comp_kind = c(A = 0.45, B = 0.45, N = 0.10),\n                         comp_age = c(adult = 0.70, child = 0.15, elder = 0.15),\n                         disabled_prop = 0.12,\n                         mode = c(\"diversity\", \"value\"),\n                         skin_tones = c(\"#4A3728\", \"#7A5230\", \"#B07A4E\",\n                                        \"#D9A066\", \"#F0C892\", \"#F5E1C8\"),\n                         value = NULL,\n                         seed = 1,\n                         dx = 1.15, dy = 2.15) {\n  mode \u003c- match.arg(mode)\n  if (identical(ncol, \"auto\")) ncol \u003c- choose_ncol(n, dx, dy, aspect)\n  nrow_ \u003c- ceiling(n / ncol)\n\n  kinds \u003c- sample_counts(n, comp_kind, seed)\n  ages \u003c- sample_counts(n, comp_age, seed + 1)\n  disabled \u003c- sample_counts(n, c(yes = disabled_prop, no = 1 - disabled_prop), seed + 2) == \"yes\"\n\n  if (mode == \"diversity\") {\n    set.seed(seed + 3)\n    fill \u003c- sample(skin_tones, n, replace = TRUE)\n  } else {\n    stopifnot(\"`value` must be supplied and length n when mode = 'value'\" =\n                !is.null(value) && length(value) == n)\n    fill \u003c- diverging_colors(value)\n  }\n\n  polys \u003c- vector(\"list\", n)\n  for (i in seq_len(n)) {\n    r \u003c- (i - 1) %/% ncol\n    c \u003c- (i - 1) %% ncol\n    ox \u003c- c * dx\n    oy \u003c- -r * dy\n    scale \u003c- if (ages[i] == \"child\") 0.65 else 1.0\n    p \u003c- person_parts(kinds[i], ages[i], disabled[i], scale, ox, oy)\n    p$icon_id \u003c- i\n    p$poly_id \u003c- paste(i, p$part)\n    p$fill \u003c- fill[i]\n    polys[[i]] \u003c- p\n  }\n  df \u003c- do.call(rbind, polys)\n\n  # No expansion/margins: the panel should be exactly the icons' bounding\n  # box, so that when Reveal stretches the image to fill the slide there\n  # is no leftover white border eating into the \"fill the window\" effect.\n  plot \u003c- ggplot(df, aes(x, y, group = poly_id, fill = fill)) +\n    geom_polygon() +\n    scale_fill_identity() +\n    coord_equal(expand = FALSE) +\n    theme_void() +\n    theme(plot.margin = margin(0, 0, 0, 0))\n\n  meta \u003c- data.frame(icon_id = seq_len(n), kind = kinds, age = ages,\n                      disabled = disabled, fill = fill)\n\n  alt_text \u003c- if (mode == \"diversity\") {\n    sprintf(\n      \"Grid of %d person icons of varying skin tone, gender presentation, and age, illustrating sample diversity. %d%% shown as children, %d%% as older adults, and %d%% using a wheelchair icon.\",\n      n,\n      round(100 * mean(ages == \"child\")),\n      round(100 * mean(ages == \"elder\")),\n      round(100 * mean(disabled))\n    )\n  } else {\n    sprintf(\n      \"Grid of %d person icons coloured on a diverging scale from %.2f to %.2f, blue for negative values, near-white at zero, orange for positive values.\",\n      n, min(value), max(value)\n    )\n  }\n\n  list(plot = plot, data = df, meta = meta, alt_text = alt_text)\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":4}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01C2FH3hCosgGfvLD7L7TbGu"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 9.1KB, 221 lines, showing lines 1-221]\n1\t#' =======================================...[truncated, total 10134 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 9.1KB, 221 lines, showing lines 1-221]\n#' ============================================================\n#' concept-map.R\n#'\n#' A general \"N columns of ellipses, many-to-many curved links between\n#' adjacent columns\" diagram, in the style of McElreath's Statistical\n#' Rethinking Figure 1.2 (theories -> process models -> statistical\n#' models). Built from plain polygons + ggplot2::geom_curve, so it\n#' needs nothing beyond ggplot2.\n#'\n#' Quarto usage (the exact figure you described):\n#'\n#'   ```{r}\n#'   #| label: fig-theory-process-stats\n#'   #| fig-cap: \"Many theories and processes can imply the same statistical result\"\n#'   source(\"concept-map.R\")\n#'\n#'   columns \u003c- list(\n#'     c(\"Words we experience more are easier to access\",\n#'       \"Experience does not matter to access\",\n#'       \"Words we experience early in life are easier to access\"),\n#'     c(\"Neural network connections change\",\n#'       \"Neural word representations change\",\n#'       \"Word recognition decisions change\"),\n#'     c(\"Experience correlates with word recognition speed\",\n#'       \"Experience does not correlate with word recognition speed\")\n#'   )\n#'   titles \u003c- c(\"Theories\", \"Process models\", \"Statistical models\")\n#'\n#'   # edges[[k]]: which item in column k links to which item in column k+1\n#'   # (1-based indices, one row per link -- repeat a `from` or `to` for\n#'   # many-to-many links)\n#'   edges \u003c- list(\n#'     data.frame(from = c(1,1,2,3,3), to = c(1,2,3,1,2)),   # theories -> processes\n#'     data.frame(from = c(1,2,3,3),   to = c(1,1,2,1))       # processes -> stats\n#'   )\n#'\n#'   concept_map(columns, edges, titles)$plot\n#'   ```\n#' ============================================================\n\nlibrary(ggplot2)\n\n# ---- geometry ------------------------------------------------------------\n\nellipse_pts \u003c- function(cx, cy, rx, ry, n = 72) {\n  t \u003c- seq(0, 2 * pi, length.out = n)\n  data.frame(x = cx + rx * cos(t), y = cy + ry * sin(t))\n}\n\nwrap_label \u003c- function(label, width = 16) {\n  paste(strwrap(label, width = width), collapse = \"\\n\")\n}\n\n# ---- main function ---------------------------------------------------\n\n#' @param columns    a list of character vectors, one per column, each\n#'                   holding that column's node labels top-to-bottom.\n#' @param edges      a list of length length(columns)-1; edges[[k]] is a\n#'                   data frame with columns `from` and `to`: 1-based\n#'                   indices into columns[[k]] and columns[[k+1]]\n#'                   respectively. Repeat an index for many-to-many links.\n#' @param titles     optional character vector of column headings.\n#' @param rx, ry     ellipse half-width / half-height. Either a single\n#'                   value (applied to every column) or a vector of\n#'                   length length(columns) giving a separate size per\n#'                   column -- useful when one column's labels are much\n#'                   longer than another's (e.g. long theory sentences\n#'                   vs. short process names).\n#' @param dx, dy     horizontal spacing between columns / vertical\n#'                   spacing between ellipses within a column. Note `dx`\n#'                   is a single fixed gap regardless of per-column `rx`;\n#'                   a warning is issued if it's too small to clear two\n#'                   adjacent columns' ellipses.\n#' @param wrap_width characters per line before wrapping label text.\n#'                   Single value or one per column, as for `rx`/`ry`.\n#' @param fill_colors  one fill colour per column; recycled/extended\n#'                   with a default pastel palette if too few given.\n#' @param curvature  passed to geom_curve (0 = straight lines).\n#' @param arrows     if TRUE (default), draw a small arrowhead at each\n#'                   curve's target end. Set FALSE for plain connecting\n#'                   lines, closer to McElreath's original figure (which\n#'                   shows correspondence between columns, not\n#'                   directional causality).\n#'\n#' @return list(plot = ggplot object, nodes = node data frame,\n#'   edges = edge data frame used for the curves)\nconcept_map \u003c- function(columns, edges, titles = NULL,\n                         rx = 1.15, ry = 0.62, dx = 3.3, dy = 1.55,\n                         wrap_width = 16,\n                         fill_colors = c(\"#F6DCC7\", \"#CFE3F5\", \"#D9E8CE\", \"#E9D8F0\", \"#F5E3B0\"),\n                         edge_color = \"#888888\", edge_alpha = 0.7, curvature = 0.2,\n                         arrows = TRUE) {\n  k \u003c- length(columns)\n  stopifnot(length(edges) == k - 1)\n  if (length(fill_colors) \u003c k) {\n    fill_colors \u003c- grDevices::colorRampPalette(fill_colors)(k)\n  }\n\n  # rx/ry/wrap_width can be a single value (applied to every column) or\n  # a vector with one entry per column.\n  recycle_per_column \u003c- function(val, name) {\n    if (length(val) == 1) return(rep(val, k))\n    if (length(val) == k) return(val)\n    stop(sprintf(\"`%s` must have length 1 or length(columns) = %d, not %d.\",\n                 name, k, length(val)))\n  }\n  rx \u003c- recycle_per_column(rx, \"rx\")\n  ry \u003c- recycle_per_column(ry, \"ry\")\n  wrap_width \u003c- recycle_per_column(wrap_width, \"wrap_width\")\n\n  # dx is a single fixed column spacing; if per-column rx makes two\n  # neighbouring ellipses wider than that gap, warn rather than silently\n  # rendering overlapping ellipses.\n  for (ci in seq_len(k - 1)) {\n    min_dx \u003c- rx[ci] + rx[ci + 1]\n    if (dx \u003c= min_dx) {\n      warning(sprintf(\n        paste(\"dx = %.2f may be too small for columns %d and %d\",\n              \"(rx = %.2f and %.2f): their ellipses may overlap.\",\n              \"Consider increasing dx.\"),\n        dx, ci, ci + 1, rx[ci], rx[ci + 1]\n      ))\n    }\n  }\n\n  # Validate edge indices up front, so a typo (e.g. an off-by-one, or a\n  # `from`/`to` swapped) fails with a message naming the column and the\n  # offending index -- rather than silently dropping the edge (as NA\n  # coordinates) later and only surfacing a generic ggplot warning.\n  for (ci in seq_len(k - 1)) {\n    e \u003c- edges[[ci]]\n    n_from \u003c- length(columns[[ci]])\n    n_to \u003c- length(columns[[ci + 1]])\n    bad_from \u003c- e$from[!(e$from %in% seq_len(n_from))]\n    bad_to \u003c- e$to[!(e$to %in% seq_len(n_to))]\n    if (length(bad_from) > 0) {\n      stop(sprintf(\n        \"edges[[%d]]$from has out-of-range value(s) %s: column %d ('%s') only has %d item(s), so valid indices are 1..%d.\",\n        ci, paste(unique(bad_from), collapse = \", \"), ci,\n        if (!is.null(titles)) titles[ci] else paste(\"column\", ci), n_from, n_from\n      ))\n    }\n    if (length(bad_to) > 0) {\n      stop(sprintf(\n        \"edges[[%d]]$to has out-of-range value(s) %s: column %d ('%s') only has %d item(s), so valid indices are 1..%d.\",\n        ci, paste(unique(bad_to), collapse = \", \"), ci + 1,\n        if (!is.null(titles)) titles[ci + 1] else paste(\"column\", ci + 1), n_to, n_to\n      ))\n    }\n  }\n\n  # node layout: column i at x = (i-1)*dx, items centred vertically,\n  # using that column's own rx/ry/wrap_width.\n  nodes \u003c- do.call(rbind, lapply(seq_len(k), function(ci) {\n    labels \u003c- columns[[ci]]\n    m \u003c- length(labels)\n    data.frame(\n      col = ci, idx = seq_len(m),\n      x = (ci - 1) * dx,\n      y = (m - 1) / 2 * dy - (seq_len(m) - 1) * dy,\n      label = vapply(labels, wrap_label, character(1), width = wrap_width[ci]),\n      fill = fill_colors[ci],\n      rx = rx[ci], ry = ry[ci],\n      stringsAsFactors = FALSE\n    )\n  }))\n  nodes$node_id \u003c- paste(nodes$col, nodes$idx)\n\n  node_xy \u003c- function(ci, i) {\n    row \u003c- nodes[nodes$col == ci & nodes$idx == i, ]\n    c(x = row$x, y = row$y, rx = row$rx)\n  }\n\n  # edge endpoints: right edge of source ellipse -> left edge of target,\n  # each using its own node's rx (so differently-sized ellipses still\n  # meet at their actual boundaries).\n  edge_df \u003c- do.call(rbind, lapply(seq_len(k - 1), function(ci) {\n    e \u003c- edges[[ci]]\n    do.call(rbind, lapply(seq_len(nrow(e)), function(j) {\n      p1 \u003c- node_xy(ci, e$from[j])\n      p2 \u003c- node_xy(ci + 1, e$to[j])\n      data.frame(x = p1[\"x\"] + p1[\"rx\"], y = p1[\"y\"],\n                 xend = p2[\"x\"] - p2[\"rx\"], yend = p2[\"y\"])\n    }))\n  }))\n\n  # ellipse polygons, each at its own node's rx/ry\n  ellipse_df \u003c- do.call(rbind, lapply(seq_len(nrow(nodes)), function(i) {\n    n \u003c- nodes[i, ]\n    e \u003c- ellipse_pts(n$x, n$y, n$rx, n$ry)\n    e$node_id \u003c- n$node_id\n    e$fill \u003c- n$fill\n    e\n  }))\n\n  title_df \u003c- NULL\n  if (!is.null(titles)) {\n    top_y \u003c- max(nodes$y + nodes$ry) + 0.6\n    title_df \u003c- data.frame(x = unique(nodes$x), y = top_y, label = titles)\n  }\n\n  plot \u003c- ggplot() +\n    geom_curve(data = edge_df, aes(x = x, y = y, xend = xend, yend = yend),\n               curvature = curvature, color = edge_color, alpha = edge_alpha,\n               linewidth = 0.6,\n               arrow = if (arrows) arrow(length = unit(0.12, \"cm\"), type = \"closed\") else NULL) +\n    geom_polygon(data = ellipse_df, aes(x, y, group = node_id, fill = fill),\n                 color = \"#555555\", linewidth = 0.4) +\n    scale_fill_identity() +\n    geom_text(data = nodes, aes(x, y, label = label), size = 3.2, lineheight = 0.9) +\n    coord_equal() +\n    theme_void()\n\n  if (!is.null(title_df)) {\n    plot \u003c- plot + geom_text(data = title_df, aes(x, y, label = label),\n                              fontface = \"bold\", size = 4.5)\n  }\n\n  list(plot = plot, nodes = nodes, edges = edge_df)\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":0}</script></tool-result></div>

## User

Further context: The trial-level redesign is in place and verified. Summary of the changes:

Model. simulate_vocab_data() now generates, per person, an ability theta (logit scale), converts it to a per-item probability p_correct = plogis(theta), and draws observed_score ~ Binomial(40, p_correct) — the vectorized equivalent of 40 independent Bernoulli trials summed. Because a binomial draw is structurally confined to {0, ..., n_items}, observed_score can never fall outside [0, 40]; I confirmed min = 1, max = 40 over 100,000 simulated scores at the default settings, with no clipping step needed anywhere.

Calibration. theta_mu and theta_sigma aren't set directly — calibrate_ability() solves for them numerically (nested uniroot over base-R integrate()) so the emergent mean score and parallel-forms reliability match your mu and reliability arguments. At the defaults (mu = 30, reliability = 0.80), this recovers a reliability of 0.80 on a large-sample check via simulate_parallel_forms() (0.8005) and an emergent sd_true ≈ 5.22, close to the old script's sd_true = 5 default, so the visual scale of the figures shouldn't shift much.

One trade-off worth flagging explicitly: sd_true is no longer an independent input. In this model, mean, reliability, and test length jointly pin down the spread — you can't set all three freely. Your main lever for spread is reliability. If you want independent control over spread later (e.g., for a "same mean, different SD" pair of figures), I can add an alternate calibration path that takes sd_true directly instead of reliability — flagging rather than building preemptively.

Correlation attenuation. r_with_x is now interpreted as the target correlation between x and the observed score (what actually shows up in plot_scatter()), not the hidden ability. Internally it's inflated by 1/sqrt(reliability) to counter attenuation, then capped at sqrt(reliability) with a warning if infeasible — verified: requesting r_with_x = 0.5 at the default reliability produced an observed correlation of 0.49 at large n, and requesting an infeasible 0.9 at reliability = 0.5 correctly warned and capped at 0.71.

Naming. The old true_score/accuracy columns are gone; the returned columns are now id, x, theta, p_correct, observed_score. Nothing in the qmd references these yet, so this is a clean rename with no breakage.

Integrated a McElreath-style concept-map figure under \"The theory is not the hypothesis...\" slide, and built a five-slide two-column sequence under \"Given the logic, the challenges: People are kind of random\" progressing from one person/one score through two people, a 20-person sample, six independent studies (icon grids), and the corresponding six score distributions (dotpile grids). There will need to be two sets of figures: (1.) a series of icon-based images progressively showing one person responding to a computer-based data collection task (this is you being tested), your score is x=y, 2 x that image (we test two people), their scores are y1, y2, a grid of x times the image (this is a sample of people), we do not show the scores but my narrative says there are multiple scores (2.) a series of synthetic data-based computationally generated figures showing one score (a dot plot), two scores (two dots), a pile of dots (this would be based on ggdist dot geom objects designed to show a probability distribution as a set of dots not a density curve or a histogram), then a histogram showing a normal distribution, then a grid of histograms to represent  multiple normal distributions. I need to be able to generate both sets of images parametrically so that (1.) the figure images should be capable of the same color or shape variation permitted previously, and I should be able to generate grids of as many figure images as required (2.) the synthetic data simulation based images should be capable of variation with respect to the mean and the SD of distributions. The basis for the synthetic data simulation is: (1.) measurement error framework y = a + bx_i + e_i where e_i can reflect measurement error, and errors e_1 ... e_n can be considered a random sample from a distribution mean mean 0 and standard deviation \\sigma. histograms\\n#' (repeated studies) -> a scatterplot against a continuous covariate.\\n#'\\n#' Model:\\n#'   True score:      T_i  = mu + sd_true * (r*z_x + sqrt(1-r^2)*z_i)\\n#'   Observed score:  Y_i  = T_i + e_i,  e_i ~ N(0, sigma_meas)\\n#'\\n#' where z_x is the covariate (e.g. age, reading experience) in\\n#' standardised form, r is the desired population correlation between\\n#' true score and the covariate (settable from -1 to 1), and\\n#' sigma_meas is chosen so that the test-retest / parallel-forms\\n#' reliability (corr between two independent measurements of the same\\n#' true score) equals `reliability` (default 0.80, per the ~80%\\n#' correlation you'd expect from two equivalent forms of a decent\\n#' vocabulary test).\\n#'\\n#' Requires: ggplot2, ggdist (for the dot-pile Author submits manuscript to editor.\n#'   2. Editor sends manuscript out to two reviewers (fan-out).\n#'   3. Reviewers submit reviews back to editor (fan-in).\n#'   4. Editor makes a decision and informs the author.\n#' A dashed loop-back arrow underneath represents \"reject with\n#' revise & resubmit -> repeat\" until accept/reject.\n#'\n#' Requires person-grid.R and testing-sequence.R sourced first\n#' (same folder).\n#'\n#' Quarto usage:\n#'   ```{r}\n#'   source(\"person-grid.R\")\n#'   source(\"testing-sequence.R\")\n#'   source(\"peer-review-workflow.R\")\n#'   peer_review_workflow()$plot\n#'   ```\n#' ============================================================\n\nlibrary(ggplot2)\n\n#' Build the peer-review workflow diagram.\n#' @return list(plot = ggplot object)\npeer_review_workflow \u003c- function() {\n\n  researcher_col \u003c- list(person = \"#3B6EA5\", screen = \"#CFE8FF\")\n  editor_col     \u003c- list(person = \"#B0562D\", screen = \"#FFDCC5\")\n  reviewer_col   \u003c- list(person = \"#3B9E5F\", screen = \"#D3F5DF\")\n\n  pos \u003c- list(\n    researcher1 = c(0,    0),\n    editor1     = c(5.0,  0),\n    reviewerA   = c(10.5, 3.4),\n    reviewerB   = c(10.5, -3.4),\n    editor2     = c(16.0, 0),\n    researcher2 = c(21.0, 0)\n  )\n\n  icon_df \u003c- function(name, kind, cols) {\n    p \u003c- pos[[name]]\n    d \u003c- person_at_computer(kind = kind, ox = p[1], oy = p[2],\n                             person_color = cols$person, screen_color = cols$screen)\n    d$icon \u003c- name\n    d\n  }\n\n  icons \u003c- rbind(\n    icon_df(\"researcher1\", \"A\", researcher_col),\n    icon_df(\"editor1\",     \"B\", editor_col),\n    icon_df(\"reviewerA\",   \"A\", reviewer_col),\n    icon_df(\"reviewerB\",   \"B\", reviewer_col),\n    icon_df(\"editor2\",     \"B\", editor_col),\n    icon_df(\"researcher2\", \"A\", researcher_col)\n  )\n  icons$poly_id \u003c- paste(icons$icon, icons$part, icons$role)\n\n  right_of \u003c- function(name, dy = 0.9) c(pos[[name]][1] + 1.25, pos[[name]][2] + dy)\n  left_of  \u003c- function(name, dy = 0.9) c(pos[[name]][1] - 0.55, pos[[name]][2] + dy)\n\n  straight_arrows \u003c- rbind(\n    data.frame(rbind(c(right_of(\"researcher1\"), left_of(\"editor1\")))),\n    data.frame(rbind(c(right_of(\"editor2\"),     left_of(\"researcher2\"))))\n  )\n  names(straight_arrows) \u003c- c(\"x\", \"y\", \"xend\", \"yend\")\n\n  curve_arrows \u003c- data.frame(rbind(\n    c(right_of(\"editor1\")[1],   right_of(\"editor1\")[2],   left_of(\"reviewerA\")[1], left_of(\"reviewerA\")[2],  0.30),\n    c(right_of(\"editor1\")[1],   right_of(\"editor1\")[2],   left_of(\"reviewerB\")[1], left_of(\"reviewerB\")[2], -0.30),\n    c(right_of(\"reviewerA\")[1], right_of(\"reviewerA\")[2], left_of(\"editor2\")[1],   left_of(\"editor2\")[2],   -0.30),\n    c(right_of(\"reviewerB\")[1], right_of(\"reviewerB\")[2], left_of(\"editor2\")[1],   left_of(\"editor2\")[2],    0.30)\n  ))\n  names(curve_arrows) \u003c- c(\"x\", \"y\", \"xend\", \"yend\", \"curvature\")\n\n  loop_arrow \u003c- data.frame(\n    x = pos$researcher2[1] + 0.35, y = -7.0,\n    xend = pos$researcher1[1] + 0.35, yend = -7.0\n  )\n\n  step_captions \u003c- data.frame(\n    x = c(pos$researcher1[1] + 0.35, pos$editor1[1] + 0.35,\n          (pos$reviewerA[1] + pos$reviewerB[1]) / 2, pos$editor2[1] + 0.35),\n    y = -6.0,\n    label = c(\"1. Submit\\nmanuscript\",\n              \"2. Editor sends\\nto review\",\n              \"3. Reviewers\\nsubmit reviews\",\n              \"4. Decision: accept /\\nR&R / reject\")\n  )\n\n  role_labels \u003c- data.frame(\n    x = c(pos$researcher1[1] + 0.35, pos$editor1[1] + 0.35,\n          pos$reviewerA[1] + 0.35, pos$reviewerB[1] + 0.35,\n          pos$editor2[1] + 0.35, pos$researcher2[1] + 0.35),\n    # Reviewer A's label needs the same +2.4 clearance from icon centre\n    # used for the on-centerline roles; the previous +1.4 offset landed\n    # on the icon itself. Reviewer B keeps its original offset since its\n    # label already clears the icon without crowding the step captions\n    # below it.\n    y = c(2.4, 2.4, pos$reviewerA[2] + 2.4, -4.8, 2.4, 2.4),\n    label = c(\"Author\", \"Editor\", \"Reviewer 1\", \"Reviewer 2\", \"Editor\", \"Author\")\n  )\n\n  plot \u003c- ggplot() +\n    geom_polygon(data = icons, aes(x, y, group = poly_id, fill = fill)) +\n    geom_segment(data = straight_arrows,\n                 aes(x = x, y = y, xend = xend, yend = yend),\n                 arrow = arrow(length = unit(0.22, \"cm\"), type = \"closed\"),\n                 linewidth = 0.6, color = \"grey30\")\n\n  for (i in seq_len(nrow(curve_arrows))) {\n    plot \u003c- plot + geom_curve(data = curve_arrows[i, ],\n                               aes(x = x, y = y, xend = xend, yend = yend),\n                               arrow = arrow(length = unit(0.22, \"cm\"), type = \"closed\"),\n                               linewidth = 0.6, color = \"grey30\",\n                               curvature = curve_arrows$curvature[i])\n  }\n\n  plot \u003c- plot +\n    geom_curve(data = loop_arrow,\n               aes(x = x, y = y, xend = xend, yend = yend),\n               arrow = arrow(length = unit(0.22, \"cm\"), type = \"closed\"),\n               linewidth = 0.5, color = \"grey55\", linetype = \"dashed\",\n               curvature = -0.15) +\n    annotate(\"text\", x = (pos$researcher1[1] + pos$researcher2[1]) / 2 + 0.35,\n             y = -7.9, label = \"reject with revise & resubmit \\u2192 repeat the cycle\",\n             size = 3.3, color = \"grey40\", fontface = \"italic\") +\n    geom_text(data = role_labels, aes(x, y, label = label), size = 3.1, color = \"grey20\") +\n    geom_text(data = step_captions, aes(x, y, label = label), size = 3.4,\n              lineheight = 0.95, fontface = \"bold\") +\n    scale_fill_identity() +\n    coord_equal(clip = \"off\", ylim = c(-8.8, 5.8)) +\n    theme_void() +\n    theme(plot.margin = margin(20, 25, 20, 25))\n\n  list(plot = plot)\n}\n"},"elapsedMs":0}</script></tool-result></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01RPQr5AemVocA4MRDoHWmcJ"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"read","input":{"file_path":"workflow-overview.qmd"},"caption":"workflow-overview.qmd","captionLink":"workflow-overview.qmd"}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01RPQr5AemVocA4MRDoHWmcJ"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 3.8KB, 75 lines, showing lines 1-75]\n1\t---\n2\ttitle: \"Quarto workflow overview\"\n3\t--...[truncated, total 4199 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 3.8KB, 75 lines, showing lines 1-75]\n---\ntitle: \"Quarto workflow overview\"\n---\n\n## The typical workflow: locating where you work\n\nMostly, we would expect to work to write or to make edits to content for a book like this book in the following workflow.\n\n- We could but we typically will not make edits to files in the repository through GitHub. Notice that the repository view shows us the files in the repository folder.\n\n![github-repository-information-file-view](/files/github-repository-information-file-view.png){#fig-github-repository-information-file-view width=60% fig-align=\"center\"}\n\nIf you click on the files e.g. the `references.bib` file then you can see the file contents and a pencil at the top of the file view window. If you clicked on the pencil, you could edit the file contents directly through the GitHub web interface.\n\n![github-repository-information-file-edit-view](/files/github-repository-information-file-edit-view.png){#fig-github-repository-information-file-edit-view width=60% fig-align=\"center\"}\n\n- We are not going to do that.\n\nMost of the time, I would expect us to work locally (on our computer):\n\n- with the contents of the local hard drive folder version of our project folder;\n- writing, or making edits, to those contents or to new content elements in the R-Studio IDE (or the new Positron IDE);\n- and ultimately pushing those local changes from our computer up to the remote repository.\n\nIn any working session, then, we would begin by opening the project folder by clicking on it in our file folder window (Finder in Macs). Here, that means clicking on the `teaching-critical-thinking.Rproj` file name.\n\n![finder-project-folder-rproj](/files/finder-project-folder-rproj.png){#fig-finder-project-folder-rproj width=60% fig-align=\"center\"}\n\nIn whatever project you are working on, the workflow to this point will ensure you have an `.Rproj` folder file to work with at the top level of the folder.\n\n- Things are changing but generally a project oriented workflow is the recommended practice:\n\n\u003chttps://tidyverse.org/blog/2017/12/workflow-vs-script/>\n\n- See this information on the New Folder from Template workflow in Positron (this is a place-holder for thinking about this workflow):\n\n\u003chttps://positron.posit.co/folder-templates.html>\n\n## The typical workflow: thinking about the sequence of actions\n\nTypically, you may proceed as follows: we assume that we are working in RStudio, in the context of the `.Rproj` folder for the project.\n\n- I may sensibly begin in the Git window in RStudio (RStudio/Git) by clicking on Pull to check if any changes have been made to the project following my last action, and to pull down those changes from the remote repository to my local version of the repository.\n- I create a file or edit a file.\n- I save the changes.\n- I commit and push the changes from my local to the remote repository.\n- Maybe I'll preview the results of the changes.\n- I cycle through these changes until I am ready to render.\n- I render.\n- I push the changed or new files resulting from the render process.\n\n## Creating or editing content\n\nWe can think about creating or editing content in terms of multiple aspects. We can distinguish these aspects though we may not always be working on all of them:\n\n- Structure -- working with parts, chapters, sections, blocks, divs and spans;\n- Content -- text, images, computation inline or in code chunks;\n- Cross-references -- to other book parts, to chapters, and to sections, figures, tables, files and other resources, and links to external webpages.\n\nInformation on book structure is here:\n\n\u003chttps://quarto.org/docs/books/book-structure.html>\n\nInformation on working with markdown in Quarto to create or edit content is here:\n\n\u003chttps://quarto.org/docs/authoring/markdown-basics.html#sec-divs-and-spans>\n\nInformation on working with R is here:\n\n\u003chttps://quarto.org/docs/computations/r.html>\n\nInformation on cross-references is here:\n\n\u003chttps://quarto.org/docs/computations/r.html>\n"},"elapsedMs":6}</script></tool-result></div>

Each doc also needs to be renderable on its own, with its own YAML header and a setup chunk sourcing dependencies via relative paths, and I should actually test-render each one after creation given the user's interest in testing production. I think I have enough to draft a solid default plan while flagging these open choices—naming, YAML config timing, code reproduction style, and mermaid diagrams—then move to finalize and request approval via ExitMode since this is now a concrete, actionable deliverable involving five files.
