<div><tool-call id="toolu_01BcU4pTMKgwcsSgLWzyXUBw"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"edit","input":{"file_path":"testing-sequence-walkthrough.qmd","old_string":"```{r}\n#| label: show-monitor-parts\n#| eval: false\nmonitor_parts \u003c- function(scale = 1, ox...[truncated, total 774 chars]","new_string":"```{r}\n#| label: show-monitor-parts\n#| eval: false\nmonitor_parts \u003c- function(scale = 1, ox...[truncated, total 2169 chars]"},"caption":"testing-sequence-walkthrough.qmd","captionLink":"testing-sequence-walkthrough.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"@@ -69,20 +69,46 @@\n #| eval: false\n monitor_parts \u003c- function(scale = 1, ox = 0, oy = 0,\n                            center_y = 1.62, x_offset = 0.80) {\n-  bezel_hw \u003c- 0.40\n-  bezel_hh \u003c- 0.34\n-  # ... bezel / screen / neck / base polygons, all offset from x_offset ...\n+  bezel_hw \u003c- 0.40; bezel_hh \u003c- 0.34; inset \u003c- 0.05\n+  neck_h \u003c- 0.10; neck_hw \u003c- 0.04; base_hw \u003c- 0.22; base_h \u003c- 0.05\n+\n+  bezel_top \u003c- center_y + bezel_hh\n+  bezel_bottom \u003c- center_y - bezel_hh\n+  neck_top \u003c- bezel_bottom\n+  neck_bottom \u003c- neck_top - neck_h\n+  base_top \u003c- neck_bottom\n+  base_bottom \u003c- base_top - base_h\n+\n+  parts \u003c- list(\n+    bezel  = data.frame(x = x_offset + c(-bezel_hw, bezel_hw, bezel_hw, -bezel_hw),\n+                         y = c(bezel_top, bezel_top, bezel_bottom, bezel_bottom)),\n+    screen = data.frame(x = x_offset + c(-bezel_hw + inset, bezel_hw - inset,\n+                                          bezel_hw - inset, -bezel_hw + inset),\n+                         y = c(bezel_top - inset, bezel_top - inset,\n+                               bezel_bottom + inset, bezel_bottom + inset)),\n+    neck   = data.frame(x = x_offset + c(-neck_hw, neck_hw, neck_hw, -neck_hw),\n+                         y = c(neck_top, neck_top, neck_bottom, neck_bottom)),\n+    base   = data.frame(x = x_offset + c(-base_hw, base_hw, base_hw, -base_hw),\n+                         y = c(base_top, base_top, base_bottom, base_bottom))\n+  )\n+  # ... offset by (ox, oy) and scale, same as person_parts() ...\n }\n ```\n \n This draws four stacked polygons (bezel, inset screen, neck, base — an\n inverted-T stand) in the **same local coordinate frame** `person_parts()`\n-uses (head centred at `x = 0, y = 1.62`). Two defaults matter:\n+uses (head centred at `x = 0, y = 1.62`), with every y-coordinate chained\n+off the previous one (`neck_top \u003c- bezel_bottom`, etc.) so the stand\n+always sits flush under the screen regardless of bezel size.\n \n-- `center_y = 1.62` — the monitor is vertically centred *on the head*, not\n-  on the whole body, so the composite reads as \"looking at a screen\"\n-  rather than \"a person standing next to an unrelated box\".\n-- `x_offset = 0.80` — a fixed horizontal gap from the body's centre line.\n+::: {.callout-note title=\"Why center_y and x_offset, specifically?\"}\n+`center_y = 1.62` vertically centres the monitor *on the head*, not on the\n+whole body, so the composite reads as \"looking at a screen\" rather than\n+\"a person standing next to an unrelated box\". `x_offset = 0.80` is a\n+fixed horizontal gap from the body's centre line — both defaults exist\n+specifically to make the pairing legible, not just to place a monitor\n+somewhere on the canvas.\n+:::\n \n ```{r}\n #| label: demo-monitor-parts\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01DiDdG12pv6khcQcifERZSA"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"edit","input":{"file_path":"testing-sequence-walkthrough.qmd","old_string":"This is the icon analogue of `sampling-distributions.R`'s\n`plot_histogram_grid()`/`plot_do...[truncated, total 524 chars]","new_string":"This is the icon analogue of `sampling-distributions.R`'s\n`plot_histogram_grid()`/`plot_do...[truncated, total 703 chars]"},"caption":"testing-sequence-walkthrough.qmd","captionLink":"testing-sequence-walkthrough.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"@@ -191,11 +191,16 @@\n `plot_histogram_grid()`/`plot_dotpile_grid()`: it calls `testing_icons()`\n once per \"study\" with an offset seed (`seed + s`), so each panel is an\n **independently drawn** sample (not the same 20 icons repeated), tags each\n-panel's polygons with a `study` column, and facets. Note `poly_id` is\n-re-prefixed with `study` — without this, icon 1 in Study 1 and icon 1 in\n-Study 2 would share a `poly_id` and `ggplot` would try to connect/group\n-them incorrectly across panels.\n+panel's polygons with a `study` column, and facets.\n \n+::: {.callout-note title=\"Why re-prefix poly_id with study?\"}\n+Without `df$poly_id \u003c- paste(df$study, df$poly_id)`, icon 1 in Study 1 and\n+icon 1 in Study 2 would share the same `poly_id`, and `ggplot`'s `group`\n+aesthetic would try to connect/group their polygons together across\n+facet panels — a subtle bug that wouldn't throw an error, just quietly\n+distort the shapes.\n+:::\n+\n ```{r}\n #| label: demo-testing-icons-studies\n #| fig-cap: \"Six independent 20-person samples ('studies')\"\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_016vynrmaVeeqLxTtWcNKzU6"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"edit","input":{"file_path":"peer-review-workflow-walkthrough.qmd","old_string":"```{r}\n#| label: show-arrow-helpers\n#| eval: false\nright_of \u003c- function(name, dy = 0.9) c(...[truncated, total 1130 chars]","new_string":"```{r}\n#| label: show-arrow-helpers\n#| eval: false\nright_of \u003c- function(name, dy = 0.9) c(...[truncated, total 2023 chars]"},"caption":"peer-review-workflow-walkthrough.qmd","captionLink":"peer-review-workflow-walkthrough.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"@@ -174,18 +174,36 @@\n left_of  \u003c- function(name, dy = 0.9) c(pos[[name]][1] - 0.55, pos[[name]][2] + dy)\n ```\n \n+::: {.callout-note title=\"Why not anchor arrows at icon centres?\"}\n Arrows are anchored just outside each icon (`+1.25`/`-0.55` from the\n icon's x-position, with a small `+0.9` vertical nudge) rather than at icon\n centres — centring the arrows would draw them straight through the icon\n-polygons. Three kinds of connections are built from these anchors:\n+polygons instead of visibly connecting two separate shapes.\n+:::\n \n+Three kinds of connections are built from these anchors — the\n+fan-out/fan-in pair is the most distinctive, since its `curvature` sign is\n+chosen deliberately rather than left at a single default:\n+\n+```{r}\n+#| label: show-curve-arrows\n+#| eval: false\n+curve_arrows \u003c- data.frame(rbind(\n+  c(right_of(\"editor1\")[1],   right_of(\"editor1\")[2],   left_of(\"reviewerA\")[1], left_of(\"reviewerA\")[2],  0.30),\n+  c(right_of(\"editor1\")[1],   right_of(\"editor1\")[2],   left_of(\"reviewerB\")[1], left_of(\"reviewerB\")[2], -0.30),\n+  c(right_of(\"reviewerA\")[1], right_of(\"reviewerA\")[2], left_of(\"editor2\")[1],   left_of(\"editor2\")[2],   -0.30),\n+  c(right_of(\"reviewerB\")[1], right_of(\"reviewerB\")[2], left_of(\"editor2\")[1],   left_of(\"editor2\")[2],    0.30)\n+))\n+names(curve_arrows) \u003c- c(\"x\", \"y\", \"xend\", \"yend\", \"curvature\")\n+```\n+\n - `straight_arrows` — plain `geom_segment()` arrows: author → editor, and\n   editor → author (submission and final decision).\n-- `curve_arrows` — `geom_curve()` arrows with an explicit `curvature`\n-  value per row (`+0.30` or `-0.30`) for the fan-out (editor → two\n-  reviewers) and fan-in (two reviewers → editor) — the sign of `curvature`\n-  controls which way each curve bows, matching the reviewer's vertical\n-  offset so the curves don't cross awkwardly.\n+- `curve_arrows` (shown above) — `geom_curve()` arrows with an explicit\n+  `curvature` value per row (`+0.30` or `-0.30`) for the fan-out (editor →\n+  two reviewers) and fan-in (two reviewers → editor) — the sign of\n+  `curvature` is mirrored to match each reviewer's vertical offset, so the\n+  four curves bow outward symmetrically rather than crossing.\n - `loop_arrow` — a single dashed `geom_curve()` well below the main row\n   (`y = -7.0`), representing \"reject, revise & resubmit\" looping back from\n   the end to the start.\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01YNAtCrXsSyjDK54tVHJUKJ"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"edit","input":{"file_path":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","old_string":"fall outside `[0, 40]` — there's no clipping step anywhere in the script,\nbecause none is ...[truncated, total 129 chars]","new_string":"fall outside `[0, 40]` — there's no clipping step anywhere in the script,\nbecause none is ...[truncated, total 499 chars]"},"caption":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","captionLink":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"@@ -31,6 +31,15 @@\n fall outside `[0, 40]` — there's no clipping step anywhere in the script,\n because none is needed.\n \n+After working through it, you should be able to:\n+\n+- explain why `mu`, `reliability`/`sd_true`, and `n_items` jointly\n+  determine the simulation rather than being three free knobs;\n+- generate a sample, a repeated-studies grid, and a covariate-correlated\n+  sample of your own;\n+- predict, roughly, how a requested `r_with_x` will be attenuated at a\n+  given `reliability`.\n+\n ## Prerequisites / assumptions\n \n - **Packages**: `ggplot2`, and `ggdist` for the dot-pile figures\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01K4zBZYCNczTUS22tQtseTk"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"edit","input":{"file_path":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","old_string":"Two consequences worth knowing: (1) `r_with_x` is specified on the\n*observed*-score scale ...[truncated, total 402 chars]","new_string":"::: {.callout-note title=\"r_with_x is on the observed scale, and has a ceiling\"}\nTwo conse...[truncated, total 513 chars]"},"caption":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","captionLink":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"@@ -224,12 +224,14 @@\n # the *observed* score ends up correlated with x at (approximately) r_with_x\n ```\n \n-Two consequences worth knowing: (1) `r_with_x` is specified on the\n-*observed*-score scale — i.e. it's (approximately) the correlation you'll\n-actually see in `plot_scatter()`, not some larger hidden value; (2) at\n-low `reliability`, only a limited range of `r_with_x` is achievable at\n-all (capped at `sqrt(reliability)`), and the function warns rather than\n-silently failing if you ask for more than that.\n+::: {.callout-note title=\"r_with_x is on the observed scale, and has a ceiling\"}\n+Two consequences follow from the attenuation logic above: (1) `r_with_x`\n+is specified on the *observed*-score scale — i.e. it's (approximately)\n+the correlation you'll actually see in `plot_scatter()`, not some larger\n+hidden value; (2) at low `reliability`, only a limited range of `r_with_x`\n+is achievable at all (capped at `sqrt(reliability)`), and the function\n+warns rather than silently failing if you ask for more than that.\n+:::\n \n ```{r}\n #| label: demo-r-with-x\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01Kgnw4XhyTFD4zXAFm2kofT"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"edit","input":{"file_path":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","old_string":"One more detail handled explicitly: `scale(x)` is undefined (`NaN`) when\n`n \u003c 2` or `x` ha...[truncated, total 301 chars]","new_string":"One more detail is handled explicitly, and is worth seeing in code rather\nthan just prose,...[truncated, total 1010 chars]"},"caption":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","captionLink":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"@@ -237,12 +237,36 @@\n cor(d_cor$x, d_cor$observed_score)\n ```\n \n-One more detail handled explicitly: `scale(x)` is undefined (`NaN`) when\n-`n \u003c 2` or `x` has zero variance, and `0 * NaN` is `NaN` in R — so the\n-standardization path is skipped entirely whenever `r_theta_x == 0`,\n-preventing an `NA` score from leaking in even at the default\n-`r_with_x = 0` with `n = 1`.\n+One more detail is handled explicitly, and is worth seeing in code rather\n+than just prose, because it was a real bug caught during development:\n \n+```{r}\n+#| label: show-nan-guard\n+#| eval: false\n+z \u003c- rnorm(n)\n+if (r_theta_x == 0) {\n+  theta_z \u003c- z\n+} else {\n+  xz \u003c- as.numeric(scale(x))\n+  if (anyNA(xz)) {\n+    warning(\"x has zero variance (e.g. all identical values); r_with_x cannot be induced. Ignoring r_with_x.\")\n+    theta_z \u003c- z\n+  } else {\n+    theta_z \u003c- r_theta_x * xz + sqrt(1 - r_theta_x^2) * z\n+  }\n+}\n+```\n+\n+::: {.callout-note title=\"A real bug this guard fixes\"}\n+`scale(x)` is undefined (`NaN`) when `n \u003c 2` or `x` has zero variance, and\n+`0 * NaN` is `NaN` in R — so multiplying it in unconditionally, even at\n+the default `r_with_x = 0`, would silently turn every `theta` (and hence\n+every score) into `NA`. This exact case is `n = 1`, i.e. the simplest\n+possible call. The fix is to skip the standardization path entirely\n+whenever `r_theta_x == 0`, rather than relying on the multiplication to\n+zero it out.\n+:::\n+\n ### Repeated studies: `simulate_studies()` and `simulate_parallel_forms()`\n \n ```{r}\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01VSBn4LWrcSE9oxExTdNUGp"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"edit","input":{"file_path":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","old_string":"| `n_items` | `40` | Number of test items. |\n| `seed` | `NULL` | Reproducibility. |\n\n## Exercises","new_string":"| `n_items` | `40` | Number of test items. |\n| `seed` | `NULL` | Reproducibility. |\n\n### C...[truncated, total 714 chars]"},"caption":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","captionLink":"sampling-distributions-walkthrough.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"@@ -369,6 +369,15 @@\n | `n_items` | `40` | Number of test items. |\n | `seed` | `NULL` | Reproducibility. |\n \n+### Common errors\n+\n+| Message | Likely cause |\n+|---|---|\n+| `reliability = ... is not achievable at mu = ...` | Requested `reliability` is too close to 1 for the given `mu`/`n_items` (implausibly large ability spread). |\n+| `sd_true = ... is not achievable at mu = ...` | Requested `sd_true` exceeds the analytic ceiling `sqrt(mu * (n_items - mu))`. |\n+| Warning: `r_with_x = ... is not achievable at reliability = ...` | Requested correlation exceeds the `sqrt(reliability)` ceiling; value was capped. |\n+| Warning: `x has zero variance...` | `x` is constant (or `n \u003c 2`); `r_with_x` can't be induced and was ignored. |\n+\n ## Exercises\n \n 1. Call `calibrate_ability(mu = 30, reliability = 0.95)` and then\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_012rCqdhkJt45z27qyD3hcFc"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"edit","input":{"file_path":"concept-map-walkthrough.qmd","old_string":"renders identically wherever Quarto builds the document.\n\n## Prerequisites / assumptions","new_string":"renders identically wherever Quarto builds the document.\n\nAfter working through it, you sh...[truncated, total 474 chars]"},"caption":"concept-map-walkthrough.qmd","captionLink":"concept-map-walkthrough.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"@@ -30,6 +30,15 @@\n that happens to use ellipses and curves rather than a graph library, so it\n renders identically wherever Quarto builds the document.\n \n+After working through it, you should be able to:\n+\n+- explain how node layout and edge computation are kept independent until\n+  the final plot;\n+- build your own N-column, many-to-many concept map for a different\n+  topic, including per-column sizing for uneven label lengths;\n+- diagnose the validation errors/warnings the script raises for bad edge\n+  indices or too-tight column spacing.\n+\n ## Prerequisites / assumptions\n \n - **Packages**: `ggplot2` only.\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

<div><tool-call id="toolu_01MsJModgUDQWY2agz1J9rz7"><script type="application/json">{"version":2,"name":"edit","input":{"file_path":"concept-map-walkthrough.qmd","old_string":"Every `from`/`to` index in `edges` is checked against the actual length of\nthe relevant co...[truncated, total 706 chars]","new_string":"::: {.callout-note title=\"Why validate indices before plotting?\"}\nEvery `from`/`to` index ...[truncated, total 1241 chars]"},"caption":"concept-map-walkthrough.qmd","captionLink":"concept-map-walkthrough.qmd","inputPreview":{"type":"code","value":"@@ -135,19 +135,39 @@\n }\n ```\n \n-Every `from`/`to` index in `edges` is checked against the actual length of\n-the relevant column *before* any plotting is attempted. This is worth\n-calling out because the failure mode it avoids is subtle: an out-of-range\n-index used directly as a lookup would otherwise silently produce `NA`\n-coordinates, and the edge would simply vanish from the rendered plot with\n-only a generic, hard-to-trace `ggplot2` warning — rather than an error\n-that names the exact column and bad index.\n+::: {.callout-note title=\"Why validate indices before plotting?\"}\n+Every `from`/`to` index in `edges` is checked against the actual length\n+of the relevant column *before* any plotting is attempted. The failure\n+mode this avoids is subtle: an out-of-range index used directly as a\n+lookup would otherwise silently produce `NA` coordinates, and the edge\n+would simply vanish from the rendered plot with only a generic,\n+hard-to-trace `ggplot2` warning — rather than an error that names the\n+exact column and bad index.\n+:::\n \n-A similar check (not shown in full here) warns if `dx` is too small for\n-two neighbouring columns' `rx` values, which would otherwise manifest as\n-visibly overlapping ellipses rather than an explicit warning pointing at\n-the cause.\n+A second check guards column spacing rather than edge indices:\n \n+```{r}\n+#| label: show-dx-check\n+#| eval: false\n+for (ci in seq_len(k - 1)) {\n+  min_dx \u003c- rx[ci] + rx[ci + 1]\n+  if (dx \u003c= min_dx) {\n+    warning(sprintf(\n+      paste(\"dx = %.2f may be too small for columns %d and %d\",\n+            \"(rx = %.2f and %.2f): their ellipses may overlap.\",\n+            \"Consider increasing dx.\"),\n+      dx, ci, ci + 1, rx[ci], rx[ci + 1]\n+    ))\n+  }\n+}\n+```\n+\n+This warns if `dx` is too small for two neighbouring columns' `rx`\n+values, which would otherwise manifest as visibly overlapping ellipses\n+with no explicit warning pointing at the cause — easy to miss if you've\n+just widened one column's `rx` for longer labels without revisiting `dx`.\n+\n ### Laying out nodes and edges: the body of `concept_map()`\n \n ```{r}\n","language":"diff"}}</script></tool-call></div>

A consequence worth knowing: columns\n-with different numbers of items are *each* centred on `y = 0`\n+the mirror-image negative value).\n+\n+::: {.callout-note title=\"Rows don't align across columns\"}\n+Columns with different numbers of items are *each* centred on `y = 0`\n independently, so a specific row position doesn't correspond to the same\n-item across columns — a 3-item column and a 2-item column don't have their\n-first items aligned. This matches the McElreath-style figure (where\n-correspondence is carried by the edges, not by row alignment) but is worth\n-checking visually for your own content.\n+item across columns — a 3-item column and a 2-item column don't have\n+their first items aligned. <div><tool-result call-id="toolu_01CkTZuwujCYgnCuLoURkmzW"><script type="application/json">{"version":1,"status":"success","result":{"type":"text","value":"[File: 16.6KB, 423 lines, showing lines 265-289]\n265\t\n266\t```{r}\n267\t#| label: show-choose...[truncated, total 1042 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 16.6KB, 423 lines, showing lines 265-289]\n\n```{r}\n#| label: show-choose-ncol\n#| eval: false\nchoose_ncol \u003c- function(n, dx, dy, aspect) {\n  ncols \u003c- seq_len(n)\n  nrows \u003c- ceiling(n / ncols)\n  grid_ratio \u003c- (ncols * dx) / (nrows * dy)\n  ncols[which.min(abs(log(grid_ratio / aspect)))]\n}\n```\n\nGiven `n` icons and a target width:height `aspect` ratio (default 16:9),\nthis brute-forces every possible number of columns from 1 to `n` and picks\nwhichever makes the resulting grid's bounding box closest to that aspect\nratio — so the figure fills a slide with minimal leftover margin, without\nyou having to hand-pick `ncol` for every `n`. It compares ratios on a *log*\nscale, so \"twice too wide\" and \"twice too tall\" count as equally bad,\nrather than the search being biased toward wide grids.\n\n### Putting it together: `person_grid()`\n\nThis is the function you actually call. It:\n\n1. Resolves `ncol` (via `choose_ncol()` if left as `\"auto\"`)."},"elapsedMs":10}</script></tool-result></div>

Resolves `ncol` (via `choose_ncol(...[truncated, total 2620 chars]"},"resultPreview":{"type":"code","value":"[File: 16.6KB, 423 lines, showing lines 289-358]\n1. Resolves `ncol` (via `choose_ncol()` if left as `\"auto\"`).\n2. Samples silhouette/age/disability composition via `sample_counts()`.\n3. Resolves fill colour — either sampled skin tones (`mode = \"diversity\"`)\n   or `diverging_colors(value)` (`mode = \"value\"`).\n4. Loops over `1:n`, computing each icon's grid row/column (and hence\n   `ox`/`oy`), calling `person_parts()` for its shape (children are drawn\n   at `scale = 0.65`), and stamping on that icon's fill colour.\n5. Row-binds every icon's polygons into one data frame and builds a single\n   `ggplot()` call — one `geom_polygon()` for the whole grid, not `n`\n   separate layers, grouped by a per-icon `poly_id`.\n6. Also returns `meta` (a tidy one-row-per-icon summary) and `alt_text` (a\n   plain-language description of the composition, generated from the same\n   sampling draws used to build the icons) — handy for `fig-alt`.\n\n```{r}\n#| label: demo-person-grid-diversity\n#| fig-cap: \"person_grid() in diversity mode\"\n#| fig-width: 7\n#| fig-height: 3\ndiversity_demo \u003c- person_grid(n = 24, seed = 11)\ndiversity_demo$plot\n```\n\n```{r}\n#| label: demo-person-grid-value\n#| fig-cap: \"person_grid() in value mode, coloured by a simulated effect size\"\n#| fig-width: 7\n#| fig-height: 3\nset.seed(2716)\nvalue_demo \u003c- person_grid(n = 24, mode = \"value\", value = rnorm(24, mean = 0, sd = 1.2))\nvalue_demo$plot\n```\n\n## Quarto usage / integration\n\nMinimal setup-chunk line:\n\n```r\nsource(\"person-grid.R\")\n```\n\nReal usage from `doing-being-science.qmd` (the diversity-sample slide):\n\n````{verbatim}\n```{r}\n#| label: fig-diversity-sample\n#| fig-cap: \"The people in any group will vary in many ways: just look around you\"\n#| fig-alt: !expr diversity_grid$alt_text\n#| fig-width: 8\n#| fig-height: 4.5\n\ndiversity_grid \u003c- person_grid(n = 125, seed = 11)\ndiversity_grid$plot\n```\n````\n\nAnd the value-mode / effect-size variant:\n\n````{verbatim}\n```{r}\n#| label: fig-effect-grid-demo\n#| fig-cap: \"People vary and how conditions or interventions affect us will vary: from strongly negative (dark blue) to null (near-white) to strongly positive (dark red) impacts\"\n#| fig-alt: !expr effect_grid$alt_text\n#| fig-width: 8\n#| fig-height: 4.5\n\nset.seed(2716)\neffect_grid \u003c- person_grid(n = 60, mode = \"value\", value = rnorm(60, mean = 0, sd = 1.2))\neffect_grid$plot\n```"},"elapsedMs":1}</script></tool-result></div>

All five walkthroughs render cleanly with the pattern applied consistently. Summary of what changed in each:

- **Learning objectives**: each doc's Aims section now closes with 3 "after working through this, you should be able to..." bullets.
- **Callouts**: converted the design-decision asides into `.callout-note` divs — e.g. the monitor's `center_y`/`x_offset` rationale and `poly_id` re-prefixing (testing-sequence), the arrow-anchoring and hand-tuned-label notes (peer-review-workflow), the `r_with_x` ceiling and the `n=1` NaN-guard bug fix (sampling-distributions), and the "rows don't align across columns" / edge-validation notes (concept-map).
- **Common errors / symptoms tables**: added next to each parameter reference, covering the actual `stopifnot`/`warning` messages each script raises (or, for the argument-free `peer-review-workflow.R`, common visual symptoms and their causes instead).
- **Most distinctive design-decision code shown explicitly**: full `monitor_parts()` body (testing-sequence), the `curve_arrows` data frame with its mirrored `curvature` signs (peer-review-workflow), the `scale()`/NaN guard (sampling-distributions), and the `dx`-vs-`rx` overlap check (concept-map) — previously only described in prose or elided with `...`.
- Also collapsed the duplicate `fig-alt` explanation in `person-grid-walkthrough.qmd`, as flagged last turn.
